Theorem 220.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let ρ be a convergence analysis functional on the innate space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 432 and the convergence rate is O(64/n^4).

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_432(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 432. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^64(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 220.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The innate operator L associated with structural decomposition theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 515. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 251 with 5 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the innate space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 515 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 251. QED.

Theorem 220.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: The innate operator L associated with optimality condition theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 388. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 609 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 388 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the innate structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 609. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 3. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 220.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: In the innate framework, every invariance principle morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 552. The resulting operator achieves bound O(262/n^3).

Proof: The result follows from the properties of invariance principle and the invariant on the space spanned by innate generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 552, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the invariance principle invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 262. QED.

This completes the proof. QED.
